Document 002: 10年期收益率单周骤降17基点！AI交易动荡重燃美债避险属性

Source PDF: all report pdf/10年期收益率单周骤降17基点！AI交易动荡重燃美债避险属性.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

AI 交易出现松动，正在重新激活美债的避险属性。上周 10 年期收益率单周骤降 17 个基点，
与科技股同步下跌的幅度为 2025 年 4 ⽉关税⻛暴以来⾸⻅。空头仓位异常集中放⼤了债市
涨势， CTA 基⾦⼤规模回补空头头⼨也成为本轮债券反弹的重要推⼿。
摘要：
上周，随着市场对头部 AI 企业前景的担忧升温，10 年期美债收益率单周下⾏约 17 个基点，纳斯
达克 100 指数同期重挫 5.5% 。⽽上⼀次收益率与科技股基准指数在同⼀周内出现如此幅度的同
步下跌，还要追溯到 2025 年 4 ⽉的关税⻛暴时期。
过去数⽉，美债在股市承压期间⼏乎失去了传统的对冲功能。如今， AI 交易的动荡令这⼀局⾯出
现转机。
AI 裂缝浮现，债市逻辑⽣变
推动本轮债市反弹的核⼼叙事，来⾃ AI 交易本⾝的松动。
Anthropic 营收增速放缓的迹象，以及 AI 云计算提供商 CoreWeave 债价的持续下跌，令市场开始
质疑：这场驱动债市⼤跌的 AI 繁荣，是否正在出现裂缝？
过去数⽉， AI 投资热潮从两个⽅向压制了美债：⼀⽅⾯，⼤规模 AI 基础设施建设提振了经济，芯

⽚需求激增加剧了通胀压⼒；另⼀⽅⾯，企业为融资 AI 项⽬⽽涌⼊信⽤债市场，与美债争夺资
⾦。据彭博数据，今年投资级企业债发⾏规模较去年同期增⻓约 29% ，科技巨头是主要发⾏
⽅。
Julius Baer 固定收益策略师 Afonso Borges 在报告中写道： “ 由 AI 主导的经济下⾏，将更接近于传
统意义上的负向需求冲击 ” ，并成为通胀下⾏的催化剂。他指出，在这类情景下， “ 中期美债应能
再度发挥有效的投资组合对冲功能 ” 。该⾏据此建议在中期久期上采取超配⽴场，并援引数据
称， 2000 年⾄ 2020 年间， 10 年期美债在股市回调期间持续录得正回报。
Natixis 北美利率策略主管 John Briggs 则从供给⻆度阐释了这⼀逻辑： “ 如果投资者发现⾃⼰持有
的 AI 相关债券正在贬值，他们购买新债的意愿就会下降。这在短期内减少了 AI 发⾏对市场的冲
击，结果是更多资⾦流向⻓端美债，对供给挤出的担忧也随之减轻。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
空头回补放⼤涨势
本轮债市反弹并⾮单纯由避险需求驱动，仓位结构同样功不可没。
据彭博报道，上周债市涨势获得了 CTA （商品交易顾问）基⾦⼤规模回补空头头⼨的助推 —— 这
些基⾦此前持有异常激进的美债空头仓位。空头回补形成的买盘，进⼀步放⼤了收益率的下⾏
幅度，也令债市的双向⻛险显著上升。
Marlborough Investment Management 投资组合经理 James Athey 援引⻢克 · 吐温的名⾔评价
道：“ 套⽤⻢克 · 吐温的话，关于美债已死的传⾔，被⼤⼤夸⼤了。 ”
加息预期降温，但分歧犹存
避险资⾦的涌⼊，也在⼀定程度上抵消了通胀数据对债市的压⼒。
上周公布的消费者价格数据⾼于预期，⼀度将市场对美联储本⽉加息的概率预期推升⾄约
50% 。但随着股市抛售加剧，这⼀概率迅速回落。

美联储主席沃什随后现⾝ IMF 年会，但除重申美联储将致⼒于实现价格稳定外，未就货币政策⾛
向或经济前景发表任何评论。市场⽬前仍预计美联储年内将再加息⼀次，并于 2027 年再度收
紧。
Standard Bank G-10 策略主管 Steven Barrow 将本轮债市反弹定性为 “ 红鲱⻥ ”—— 即误导性信
号。他指向前⽅的通胀⻛险，以及资⾦从主权债券向股票资产⻓期迁移的结构性趋势。
上周五油价的急涨同样提⽰了⻛险：受伊朗战争局势推动，油价飙升重燃通胀担忧，美债与美
股⼀度同步⾛弱 —— 特朗普总统推动俄罗斯柴油进⼊全球市场的协议遭遇质疑，令原油价格在
午盘前后骤然跳升。
⻓期投资者的视⻆
对于短期交易者⽽⾔，前景充满不确定性。
State Street Investment Management 东京⾼级固定收益策略师 Masahiko Loo 指出， Warsh 不提
供前瞻指引的货币政策⻛格 —— 这是其政策框架的核⼼原则 —— 令市场更容易出现⼤幅价格波
动。
但他同时表⽰，对于⻓期投资者⽽⾔， “ 情况有所不同 ” 。“ 如果出现更⼤规模的 AI 驱动⻛险规避

⾏情，美债看起来越来越有能⼒提供投资者过去⼏年⼀直缺失的对冲功能。 ”

ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や

賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持

先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

（注）ブレーク・イーブン・インフレ率は、名目国債利回りと物価連動国債（TIPS）実質利回りの
差で、市場が織り込む期待インフレ率の目安。実質予想短期金利はNY連銀が算出する

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性

日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい
8

（出所）IMF ”World Economic Outlook”(October 2026)より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される
（出所） 日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様
（出所） 内閣府「景気ウォッチャー調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
景気ウォッチャー調査の概要
家計動向関連
（現状判断DI）
企業動向関連
（現状判断DI）
家計・企業動向関連
（先行き判断DI）
サービス消費は持ち直しも
消費全体は緩慢な回復
需要は堅調だが
資材高などの供給制約が重石に 家計・企業ともに先行き判断は慎重

金利上昇による企業の負担増加は 
いつ本格化するか

○ 日銀の政策金利や、金融市場で決まる国債金利に比べ、企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇はまだ
限定的。企業の新規借入金利や負債残高全体への影響波及に時間がかかることが背景 
○ 金融機関の新規貸出金利は、概ね3カ月かけて政策金利の変化を反映する傾向。特に地域金融機関
で上昇がやや遅く、企業規模により有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが異なる一因に 
○ 借入・社債金利の動きを踏まえると、企業の利払い負担増加は2026～2028年度に本格化し、年間
約3兆円の利益下押し要因に。これまで以上に資本効率を意識した経営が求められる

１．政策金利や国債金利が上昇するも、企業の利払い負担増加はまだ本格化せず 
日本銀行は2026年9月の金融政策決定会合で、 政策金利を1.0％から1.25％へ引き上げた。これは1995
年以来、31年ぶりの高水準である1。また、金融市場で決まる10年国債金利（いわゆる長期金利）は、
2026年9月に節目となる3％を超えた。こちらも1996年以来30年ぶりの高さであり、政策金利や国債金
利はまさに歴史的な転換点を迎えている。 
一方で、 企業が実際に負担する金利の上昇は今のところ限定的だ。 図表1は、 企業の有利子負債利子
率 （＝支払利息等÷有利子負債残高） を指標金利 （政策金利、10年国債金利）と比較したものである。
図表 1 企業の有利子負債利子率と指標金利 図表2 企業の資金繰り判断 DI

（注） 有利子負債利子率は、 支払利息等を有利子負債 （短期 ・
長期借入金＋社債＋受取手形割引残高）の期首・期末
平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業・
全規模 。凡例中の数値は、マイナス金利政策解除前
（2023年10～12月期）と2026年4～6月期の差

### Page 2

2

図中の凡例にある数字は、 各金利についてマイナス金利政策解除 （2024年3月19日 ）前 の2023年10～12
月期と、直近の2026年4～6月期を比較した値だ。政策金利が＋0.93％Pt、10年国債金利が＋1.80％Pt
と大きく上昇した一方で、企業の有利子負債利子率は＋0.42％Ptと上昇幅が小さく、企業の利払い負
担の増加はまだ本格化していない。実際、日銀短観における企業の資金繰りに関する調査（図表2）を
みると、「楽である」の回答率から「苦しい」の回答率を差し引いたDIが、2021年以降プラス圏で概
ね横ばいを維持している。企業の実感としても、これまでに実施された複数回の利上げや、金融市場
における国債金利の大幅な上昇は、まだ資金調達環境の悪化につながっていないようだ。 
なぜ、こうしたギャップが生じるのか。主な要因として考えられるのは次の2点である。 
第一の要因は、指標金利と企業の新規調達金利とのタイムラグだ。政策金利などの指標金利が変化
してから、企業が新たに資金を調達する際の金利が変化するまで、少し時間がかかる。特に、企業の
有利子負債の半分強を占める金融機関からの借入において（図表3）、新規調達金利のタイムラグが生
じやすいと考えられる。 
第二の要因は、企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグだ。企業が
新たに資金調達する際の金利が上昇しても、それが有利子負債残高全体にすぐ反映されるわけではな
い。 企業の金融機関借入金の4～5割程度が固定金利型であるほか2、 主に大企業が発行する社債の多く
が固定利付債であり、過去の低金利時代に調達した負債がまだ残っているためだ。 
本稿では、 これら2つの要因を踏まえて、政策金利・国債金利などの指標金利上昇を受けた企業の利
払い負担増加が、いつ本格的に現れるかを試算する3。 
２．金融機関からの新規借入金利は、地域金融機関で上昇ペースが遅い傾向 
まず、第一の要因（指標金利から企業の新規調達金利へのタイムラグ）の影響を検証しよう。図表
3に示したように、 企業の有利子負債には金融機関などからの借入金と、 主に大企業が発行する社債が
ある。このうち、社債を新規発行する際の金利は概ね国債金利に連動して決まり、タイムラグがほぼ
図表 3 企業の有利子負債残高の内訳 図表 4 金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利

含まれるが、 シェアが小さい （0.1％程度） ため表示し
ていない。

3

無いため、ここでの検証は金融機関などから新規に借り入れる際の金利が対象になる。 
図表4は、金融機関の新規貸出金利（金融機関からみた貸出）を、業態別に示したものだ。都市銀行
では新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが速い一方、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では政策金利の上昇に比べて新
規貸出金利の上昇ペースがやや遅い。こうした地域金融機関における金利上昇の遅れが、指標金利と
企業の新規調達金利のタイムラグに影響していると考えられる。 
金融機関の業態によって新規貸出金利の上昇ペースに差があるのは、貸出の金利形態別シェアが異
なることが一因であろう。大手行では企業向け貸出全体の約6割を占める変動金利型貸出のうち、ほと
んどが「市場金利連動」であるため、金融市場の変化による影響がすぐに反映されやすい。他方で、
地方銀行や信用金庫では変動金利型貸出のうち「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが高い。短期プ
ライムレートとは、各金融機関が設定する企業向け貸出の最優遇金利である。政策金利の変更などを
反映して決められることが多いが、各金融機関の競争環境などによって反映時期・度合いに差が生じ
る4。これにより、第二地方銀行や信用金庫では都市銀行に比べて新規貸出金利の上昇ペースが遅くな
る傾向があると考えられる5。 
こうした金融機関業態別の違いは、企業サイドにおいて、有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが規模別
に異なる一因になっている可能性がある。足元の金利上昇局面では、都市銀行からの借入が多いと考
えられる大企業（資本金10億円以上）で有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースが速く、信用金庫など地域金
融機関からの借入が多いと考えられる小規模企業（同1千万円未満）で上昇ペースが遅い（図表5）。
2010年代の金利低下局面では大企業の有利子負債利子率が小規模企業を下回る傾向にあったが、足元
では完全に逆転している。大企業は社債が有利子負債残高の約3分の1を占めるため、金融機関の新規
貸出金利のみが原因とは言えないものの、金融機関業態別の違いが企業規模別の資金調達環境に影響
を及ぼしている可能性がある点は興味深い。 
では、今後日銀がさらに利上げを進めていくと、金融機関の新規貸出金利はどの程度のペースで、
どこまで上昇していくか。過去の政策金利と新規貸出金利の関係を統計的に分析すると、 政策金利1％

平均値で除した値、対象は金融・保険を除く全産業

Ptの上昇は新規貸出金利を当月に0.31％Pt押し上げ、その後さらに3カ月間かけて当月からの累計で
0.85％Pt押し上げる傾向があることが明らかになった。日銀が今後2027 年半ばにかけて政策金利を
2.0％まで引き上げていくことを前提にすると6、金融機関の新規貸出金利（2026年8月時点で1.78％）
は2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度へ上昇すると試算される （図表6） 。 マイナス金利政策の解除前と
比較すると、2％Pt弱の上昇になる計算だ。 
３．企業の利払い負担増加は今後 2028 年度にかけて本格化。 利益を年間約 3 兆円下押し 
次に、第二の要因（企業の新規調達金利と有利子負債残高全体の利払い負担とのタイムラグ）の影
響を検証しよう。金融機関の新規貸出金利や社債の新規発行金利の上昇が、それぞれの残高全体にど
の程度のペースで反映されるかを確認し、企業の利払い負担がいつ本格化に増加するか試算する。 
金融機関の新規貸出金利と貸出残高全体の金利を比較すると、今回の金利上昇局面では新規貸出金
の上昇に対して貸出金利残高全体の金利上昇がやや遅れている（図表7）。 先に述べたように、金融機
関の貸出の一部が固定金利型であり、新規貸出や借り換えに伴って新規貸出金利の上昇が残高全体へ
波及するのに時間がかかるためだ。 
両者の過去の関係を統計的に分析すると、新規貸出金利の上昇がおよそ5年程度かけて残高全体の金
利を緩やかに押し上げる傾向があるとの結果になった。 図表6で試算したように、 先行きの新規貸出金
利が2027年秋ごろにかけて2.65％程度まで上昇していくと想定すると、貸出残高全体の金利（2026年
8月時点で1.51％ ）は2027年末に2.0％程度、2028年末に2.3％程度と緩やかに上昇し、2030年代前半に
は新規貸出金利と同じ2.65％程度へ収束していく計算になる。 
同様に社債について、 新発債金利と既発債金利を比較したものが図表8だ。 新発債金利は2022年ごろ
から上昇して足元では2％台後半に達しているが、既発債金利はまだ1％強にとどまっており、金融機
関の貸出金利に比べると残高全体への反映に時間がかかりやすいことがうかがえる。なお、日本で新
図表 7 金融機関の貸出金利の試算 図表 8 社債金利の試算

（注） 国内銀行計（除く信用金庫）の値。企業向け以外の貸
出も含む。先行きは、貸出金利（新規）を政策金利に
よる分布ラグモデルで、 貸出金利 （残高） を自己ラグ ・
貸出金利（新規）による動学誤差修正モデルで試算 
（出所）日本銀行「貸出約定平均金利の推移」より、みずほ総
合研究所調査部作成 
（注） 利払繰延条項付社債・永久社債を除く発行額加重平均
値。実績は、銘柄別情報に基づき、みずほ総合研究所
調査部が集計。 先行きは、 新発債金利を5年国債金利予
測値＋スプレッド過去平均値で、既発債金利を借り換
え＋新規発行による新発債金利反映モデルで試算

新発債金利
既発債金利
（年）

### Page 5

5

規に発行される社債の平均年限は現在5～6年であり、新発債金利は概ね5 年国債金利に連動して動く
傾向があることから、先に述べたように指標金利→新発債金利のタイムラグはほとんど生じない。 
今後、社債残高が物価上昇にあわせて年間＋2％のペースで増加していくとの前提を置き、 先行きの
償還に伴う借り換えや追加的な新規発行が行われていく場合、既発債金利は2030年代初めにかけて年
間＋0.3％Pt程度の緩やかなペースで上昇していくと試算される（新発債金利は5年国債金利の先行き
予測値に連動すると想定）。7年債や10年債といった年限が長めの社債も一定の規模があることから、
新発債金利の上昇が既発債金利に反映されるまでに時間を要する結果になった。 
最後に、金融機関の貸出金利（企業からみた借入金利7）と社債金利の試算結果を用いて、企業の有
利子負債利回りへの影響を検証する。企業の有利子負債に占める借入と社債の金額シェアを先行き一
定として、 貸出金利と社債金利の変化を有利子負債利子率に反映したものが図表9だ。2023～2025年度
の有利子負債利子率の上昇ペースは年間＋0.1～＋0.2％Pt程度と緩やかだったが、2026～2028年度に
は同＋0.3～＋0.4％Ptに加速すると見込まれる。企業の有利子負債の多くを占める借入金の金利上昇
が、主な押し上げ要因だ。2029年以後は借入金の金利上昇が落ち着く一方、社債金利の上昇継続を受
け、有利子負債利子率は2030年代にかけて緩やかに3％程度の水準まで上昇していくだろう。 
このように、金利上昇による企業の利払い負担増加は今後2028年度にかけて本格化していく見込み
だ。企業全体の有利子負債残高が先行き年間＋2％のペースで拡大していくと仮定すると、 支払利息等
が年間約3兆円増加する計算であり、経常利益（金融・保険を除くベースで2025年度：124.8兆円）に
対し年間2.4％の押し下げ要因に相当する8。 
今後、有利子負債利子率が上昇していく過程では、それに見合った利益率を達成できるかが問われ
るだろう。2025年度時点では投下資本利益率（ROIC、3.70％）の有利子負債利子率（1.45％）に対す
るスプレッドが2％Ptを上回っており、ROICでみた企業の資本効率は外部資金調達コストを十分カバー
できている（図表10）9。しかし、 有利子負債利子率が今後3％程度へ上昇していくと、ROICが現在の水
準のままではスプレッドがコロナ禍初期の2020年度並みに縮小し、企業の利払い負担に対する利益の
図表 9 企業の有利子負債利子率の試算 図表 10 有利子負債利子率と投下資本利益率

（注） 有利子負債利子率の対象は、金融 ・ 保険を除く全産業 ・
全規模。先行きは、法人企業統計調査における借入金
残高と社債残高のシェアを一定として、有利子負債利
子率への影響を試算 
（出所）財務省、日本銀行、日本証券業協会より、みずほ総合

期首・期末平均値で除した値

### Page 6

6

余力が弱まってしまう。日本の金利上昇が一段と進み、企業の利払い負担が本格的に増加していくと

［参考文献］ 
みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）「2026年・2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ～転換点は近いのか、 投資
主導経済の行方を探る～」、2026年10月2日

1 現在の政策金利は無担保コール翌日物金利の誘導目標だが、1995 年時点では公定歩合を政策金利と見なして時系列比較してお
り、厳密には政策金利の意味するものが異なる。 
2 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2026年4 月）の図表Ⅲ-1-12 によれば、法人向け貸出のうち、大手行で約40％、地域銀
行（地方銀行、第二地方銀行）や信用金庫で 45～50％程度が固定金利貸出である（2025 年 9 月末時点）。 
3 金利上昇による企業への影響には、利払いの増加によるデメリットだけでなく、利子収入の増加によるメリットもある。本稿
では、そのうちデメリットの面だけに焦点を当てて分析しており、メリットについては稿を改めて論じたい。 
4 日本銀行「金融システムレポート」 （2025年4 月）の図表Ⅵ-1-8 では、地域銀行を対象に、2024 年7 月の政策金利上昇を受け
た短期プライムレートの引き上げタイミング、引き上げ幅、引き上げ後金利の貸出先適用割合について分析が行われている。 
5 図表 4 に示した金融機関業態別の新規貸出金利には、企業向け以外の貸出も含まれる。したがって、企業向け貸出における
「市場金利連動」 、 「短期プライムレート連動」のシェアが業態別に異なることだけでなく、貸出全体に占める住宅ローン貸出
のシェアが業態別に異なることなども、新規貸出金利の上昇ペースの違いに影響していると考えられる。 
6 みずほ総合研究所調査部（2026）の見通しに基づく想定。 
7 金融機関の貸出金利には企業向け以外の貸出も含まれるが、ここでは簡易的に、企業向け貸出金利が貸出全体の金利に連動す
ると仮定して試算した。 
8 本稿では金利上昇による利払いの増加のみを計算対象としているため、利子収入の増加も考慮すれば経常利益への下押し圧力
は緩和すると考えられる。 
9 投下資本利益率（ROIC）と本来比較すべきは、負債だけでなく自己資本のコストも考慮した加重平均資本コスト（WACC）だ
が、本稿では金利上昇の影響を直接評価する観点から、投下資本利益率と有利子負債利子率を比べている。

Document 081: 261006_daiwa_kinsus

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Kinsus's leadership team comprises experienced professionals dedicated to steering the
company towards sustainable growth. This diverse board composition ensures a comprehensive
range of expertise and perspectives, contributing to Kinsus’s strategic decision-making and
corporate governamce.
Capital management 2
Kinsus maintains a robust financial framework, ensuring the availability of resources necessary
for operational efficiency and strategic investments. The company's financial health is regularly
disclosed through annual reports, providing transparency to stakeholders.
Related party &
transaction 1
The company adheres to stringent corporate governance policies to manage related party
transactions, ensuring transparency and compliance with regulatory standards. The company
discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder
trust. For instance, the company's annual general meeting documents provide details on related
party transactions, ensuring transparency and accountability.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Kinsus is committed to sustainable supply chain practices, emphasizing environmental, social,
and governance considerations. The company has established comprehensive ESG policies,
including commitments to labor standards, environmental health and safety, ethics, and conflict-
free minerals. These policies guide the company's interactions with suppliers and partners,
promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.
E Data security 3
The company recognizes the importance of information security in its operations. As part of its
corporate governance framework, Kinsus is likely to implement standard data protection
practices to safeguard sensitive information and ensure compliance with relevant regulations.
Stakeholders are encouraged to consult the company's official communications or contact the
company directly for detailed information on data security policies.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

during Aug, resulting in 3Q26 revenues up 74% YoY to NT$843m. With the gradual ramp 
up of clients’ new platform, we expect to see sequential MoM growth ahead, with Oct/ 
Nov revenues up 17%/ 19% MoM to NT$341m/ NT$406m, driven by a rising 
contribution from the LEO satellites.

Earnings revision: We factor in UMT 3Q26 revenues and revise down earnings by 1%/ 
1%/ 1% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues due to the momentum of product 
delivery, while we remain positive on the rising LEO satellite business exposure ahead.

Valuation: We continue to derive our target price based on a discounted P/E, and based 
on 2029E (methodology unchanged). We continue to derive the target P/E multiple from 
the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the satellite supply chain, and we remove outliers 
(telecom operators who face competition from LEO satellite operators). Our target 
2029E discounted P/E multiple is at 37.1x (vs. previously at 37.0x) which is based on 
peers’ avg. PEG&M, and UMT’s 2029-30E avg. NI YoY and OPM, due to higher avg. OPM 
in 2029-30E at 42% (vs. previously at 41%). We apply the 37.1x multiple to 2029E EPS 
and discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 7.8% (no change). Our 12m target price is at 
NT$2,549 (vs. previously at NT$2,557), implying 61x 2027E P/E, which is above the 
company’s average +1 stv. P/E of 40x, reflecting our positive view on UMT’s product mix 
upgrade toward LEO satellites. Maintain Buy.

### Page 4

Price target risks and methodology - UMT 
Valuation: We use a discounted P/E methodology and apply a 37.1x target P/E multiple 
to UMT’s 2029E EPS, discounting it back to 2027E at a COE of 7.8% (beta 1.2x, risk-free 
rate 1.6% and market risk premium at 5.1%), which leads to our 12-month target price 
of NT$2,549. The target P/E is based on the average PEG&M ratio of peers in the 
satellite supply chain. We are Buy-rated on UMT.  
Key Risks: Slower-than-expected LEO satellite deployment; Potential competition from 
new-entrant suppliers; LEO satellite operators manufacturing in-house components.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that

### Page 1

法国爆发全国性暴⼒抗议，全国逾 55 万⼈上街，多地市政厅及商铺遭毁焚， 2397 ⼈被捕，
受损⾦额达数亿欧元。暴动由学⽣抗议蔓延⾄⻩背⼼与农⺠，核⼼⽭盾在于公共债务占
GDP 达 119% 带来的⾼通胀与财政紧缩。受中东冲突与债市承压影响，法国国债收益率飙
升，⾯临严重的政治与财政双重危机。
摘要：
10 ⽉ 17 ⽇傍晚六点，法国中南部⼩城利摩⽇，⼀群戴⾯罩的⼈撞开了市政厅的⼤⻔。这座建筑
已经站了 143 年，钟楼⾼ 43 ⽶。两个⼩时后，钟楼在⽕光⾥塌了下来。
当天深夜，利摩⽇市政府的社交账号只发了⼀句话：
同⼀时间，巴黎共和国⼴场上的汽⻋还在烧。⻢赛有⼈⽤隔离护栏撞开市政厅⼤⻔，在墙上喷
了⼀个⽆政府主义的 "A" ，⼈群⾥有⼈⾼喊 " 我们攻占巴⼠底了 " 。⾥昂的旅游局⽼楼被点着，波
尔多的警察局被砸， CNews 和 Europe 1 的办公室玻璃碎了⼀地。从巴黎到⻢赛、尼斯、南特、
" 今晚，我们的市政厅被⼤⽕吞噬。和你们每个⼈⼀样，我惊恐地看着这座属于利摩⽇全体⼈⺠

的地⽅化为灰烬。 "

### Page 2

波尔多、⾥尔、图卢兹、斯特拉斯堡，超市、药店、眼镜店、⼿机店，还有爱⻢仕和路易威登
的橱窗，⼀个接⼀个被砸开。巴黎市⻓格雷⽠尔说，⾸都 " 整条整条的街道被毁了 " 。
内政部事后给出的数字是：全国 55 万⼈上街，巴黎 11 万。事前，他们预计最多 17 万⼈。
法国总⼯会的说法是 80 万⼈、 1750 个集会点。截⾄ 10 ⽉ 18 ⽇， 2397 ⼈被捕， 997 名警察、宪兵
和消防员受伤，其中 7 ⼈重伤。法国零售与贸易联合会统计，约 1500 家商⼾受损，损失以亿欧元
计。⼀位 78 岁的巴黎居⺠站在街垒残骸旁对法新社说： " 感觉我们像打了⼀场仗。 "
如果只把这些画⾯当成 " 法国⼈⼜上街了 " ，会错过此暴动真正的分量。街上其实挤着五拨互不相
识的⼈：⾼中⽣、⼤学⽣、⼯会成员、农⺠，以及⼋年前那场 " ⻩背⼼ " 运动的⽼⾯孔。他们喊的
⼝号不⼀样，要的东西也不⼀样。
把这五拨⼈黏在⼀起的，是⼀个数字： 119% 。
⼀把⽕从⾼中教室烧到市政厅，只⽤了三个星期
故事起点在巴黎郊区的克雷泰伊。9 ⽉中旬，⼀所⾼中的学⽣最先⾛出校⻔，理由是校舍年久失

修、班级太挤、⽼师不够。抗议随后蔓延到波尔多、雷恩、南特、⾥昂、⾥尔、⻢赛，约 25 万
学⽣卷⼊， 500 多所⾼中停课。
政府的反应不算慢：涨注册费的计划撤了， 3000 名代课教师派下去了，还开了⼀个全国线上问
卷，专⻔听学⽣说话。为平息事态，警⽅⼀度暂停在校园抗议中使⽤震爆弹。
问题在于，法国 2026 年教育部的预算是 645 亿欧元，听着不少，可其中约 285 亿要拿去付退休教
师的养⽼⾦。钱在账上，进不了教室。
在贫困社区教法语的罗歇 - 拉康打了个⽐⽅：⽔池在漏⽔，该修的是池⼦，光往⾥加⽔没⽤。
学⽣更焦虑的，是漏⽔的天花板之外的东西：那张⽂凭还能不能换来⼀份稳定的⼯作。这个焦
虑， 3000 名代课教师回答不了。

### Page 3

于是 " ⻩背⼼ " 回来了。这个 2018 年因为燃油税上街的运动，⼋年之后重新出现在队伍⾥。它的代
表⼈物罗德⾥格斯在抗议前发了⼀段视频： " ⾼中⽣在为他们的未来⽽战，⼯⼈在为靠劳动⽣活
⽽战，退休者在为尊严⽽战。我们的路不同，但我们的担忧可以汇合。 "
10 ⽉ 17 ⽇，戴着⻩⾊背⼼的⾯具抗议者在巴黎共和国⼴场附近。图源：路透社
农⺠的加⼊更直接。中东战事推⾼柴油和化肥价格，夏天⼜赶上极端天⽓，法国农业部估计约 3
万家农场急需援助。拖拉机开进城，和⾼中⽣站在了同⼀条街上。
爱丁堡⼤学当代史教授沙巴尔对半岛电视台说，这是 " ⼀系列抗议的逻辑结果 " ，⽽且把学⽣的怨
⽓和全社会的怨⽓合到了⼀起。对法国政府来说， " 这是最坏的结果 " 。
对抗也在升级。⾃ 9 ⽉中旬以来，超过 6700 名未成年⼈被警⽅拘留。在北部朗斯，⼀枚震爆弹在
学校外爆炸， 15 岁的少年失去了⼀只⼿；在西部图尔， 16 岁的学⽣被橡㬵⼦弹击中，失明；在
圣艾蒂安， 17 岁男孩的右⼿被防暴雷炸断。
这些画⾯在社交平台上反复播放，然后被复制。到 10 ⽉ 17 ⽇，原本 " 改善教学条件 " 的诉求，已经

变成了墙上的 " 向富⼈征税 "" 吃掉富⼈ " ，和街头的 " ⻢克⻰辞职 " 。内政部⻓努内斯在校园抗议中
暂停震爆弹的⼏天后，⼜授权警⽅在 " 城市暴⼒ " 中使⽤它。

### Page 4

⼈们聚集在⾥昂旅游办公室前，该办公室于 2026 年 10 ⽉ 17 ⽇⽰威中被纵⽕。图源：法新社
爱丽舍宫盯着的，是债券市场那块屏幕
街头的⽕，⻢克⻰⻅过。 2018 年⻅过， 2023 年⻅过。这⼀次真正让他和总理勒科尔尼睡不着
的，是另⼀块屏幕。
9 ⽉ 18 ⽇，欧洲评级机构 Scope 把法国主权信⽤评级从 AA- 下调到 A+ ，与惠誉、标普站到了同⼀
档。同⼀天，法国 10 年期国债收益率⼀度冲到 4.57% ， 2008 年以来最⾼；法国国债相对德国国
债的利差达到 105 个基点， 14 年来第⼀次越过 100 这道关⼝。
10 ⽉ 1 ⽇， 2027 年预算案公布当天， 10 年期收益率摸到 4.96% ， 2002 年 7 ⽉以来最⾼，离 5% 只
差⼀步。到 10 ⽉初，法德利差⼀度扩⼤到 133 个基点， 2012 年欧债危机以来最阔。
⼀个曾经和德国⼀起构成欧元区 " ⽆⻛险核⼼ " 的国家，现在的借钱成本已经⾼于希腊、意⼤利、
西班⽛和葡萄⽛。
法国国家统计与经济研究所的数据显⽰，截⾄今年⼆季度末，法国公共债务 3.596 万亿欧元，占

GDP 的 119% 。财政部⾃⼰的预测更差： 2026 年 119.3% ， 2027 年 121.7% ，是欧盟 60% 上限的两
倍。⾚字率 2025 年为 5.1% ， 2026 年预计升到 5.4% ，⽐原定的 5.0% ⽬标⾼出⼀截，那个⽬标实
际上已经被放弃；经济增⻓预期今年已被下调三次，从 0.7% 降到 0.5% 。
更⿇烦的是利息。财政部⻓莱斯库尔在公布预算时说，明年仅付息就要吃掉⾚字的⼀半以上，
到本⼗年末会达到 1000 亿欧元。他说完这句，⼜补了⼀句 " 法国的签名是可靠的 " 。同⼀天，他在
考虑另⼀件事：少发⻓债，多发短债。
投标倍数的变化⽐官员的表态更诚实。法国⻓期国债的投标倍数从 2025 年的 3.2 掉到 2.5 ，短期
国债从 3.2 掉到 2.1 ；发⾏的平均期限在 6 ⽉已缩到 5 年，⽐ 2024 年初短了半年。买家在变短，也
在变挑。
法国的问题不是债务太⾼，⽽是没有⼈能决定怎么还。
希腊债务⽐法国⾼，意⼤利也⽐法国⾼。可希腊已经削过⼀轮，意⼤利⾄少有⼀个稳定的政

府。法国是四件事同时发⽣：⾼债务、低增⻓、碎⽚化的议会、半年后的⼤选。
五⼗三年没平衡过预算，法国⽋钱不是从今天开始的

### Page 5

把时间拉⻓，会看到另⼀幅图景。
法国上⼀次实现预算平衡是 1973 年，上⼀次达到欧盟 3% ⾚字上限是 2019 年。最近三年，⾚字率
⼀直在 5% 以上。
2017 年⻢克⻰刚上台时，法国公共债务还不到 GDP 的 100% ，⾚字率 2.8% 。当时他被视为那个
能把法国 " 改好 " 的⼈。
2018 年冬天， " ⻩背⼼ " 烧了⾹榭丽舍，政府撤回了燃油税。 2020 年疫情，法国推出 " ⽆论代价如
何 " 的全⾯兜底。 2023 年退休年龄从 62 岁推到 64 岁，全国抗议持续数⽉。 2024 年欧洲议会选举
后解散国⺠议会，换来⼀个没有任何⼀党过半的议会。当年 12 ⽉，巴尼耶政府因预算被推翻；
2025 年，⻉鲁政府重演了同⼀幕。勒科尔尼是第三个。
每⼀次危机，法国都⽤借债买了⼀次平静。平静越来越短，账单越来越⻓。
现在法国的公共⽀出占 GDP 的 57% 。福利、公务员⼯资、医保、养⽼⾦，每⼀项都是刚性的。
想削减，街上就着⽕；不削减，债市就着⽕。世界报⽤ " 经济陷阱正在收紧 " 形容这个局⾯：⼀边

要增加投资推动再⼯业化，⼀边要削减不断侵蚀财政空间的⾚字，两边都要钱。
中东的战争，正好打在法国最软的地⽅
债务是过去五⼗三年⼀层层攒下来的。今年的⿇烦，是在这个存量上⼜新添了⼀层。
今年 2 ⽉ 28 ⽇，中东战事爆发。霍尔⽊兹海峡被卡住，这条通道承载全球约 20% 的碳氢化合物消
费。
法兰西银⾏ 6 ⽉的《⾦融稳定报告》给了⼀个很克制的判断：这⼀次和 2022 年不⼀样。油⽓库存
⽐当年⾼得多，东亚需求⼤幅收缩，液化天然⽓的冲击远⼩于俄乌战争之后，所以原油价格涨
得 " 相对温和 " 。
成品油不讲这个道理。欧洲柴油价格较年初翻了⼀倍，法国柴油零售价创下每升 2.41 欧元的纪
录，德国是 2.471 欧元。今年 3 ⽉到 8 ⽉，中东柴油出⼝从每天 80 万桶掉到 40 万桶，⽽ 2025 年中
东满⾜了欧洲四成以上的柴油进⼝。
9 ⽉，欧元区通胀率升到 3.8% ， 8 ⽉还是 3.2% ，能源价格同⽐涨 18.8% 。法国的 CPI 从 8 ⽉的
2.4% 跳到 9 ⽉的 3.1% 。

对普通家庭来说，前⼏年政府发的那些补贴，基本被物价吃⼲净了。
法国超过 60% 的进⼝柴油要经过霍尔⽊兹海峡和苏伊⼠运河，约 15% 的原油来⾃中东。⼀条航
道⾛不通，加油站⻔⼝就开始排队。
最要命的⼀环藏在财政合同⾥。法国⼤约⼗分之⼀的债务是指数化债券，通胀⼀起来，这部分
债务的本⾦⾃动变⼤；养⽼⾦和家庭补贴也按通胀上调。收⼊端呢，燃油税按销量收，油价⼀
⾼，销量下来，税收反⽽少了。
通胀每多拖⼀个季度，法国的债务就⾃动多背⼀层。
法兰西银⾏算过账：按 4 ⽉的数据，这场战争会让 2026 年的财政平衡多出 60 亿欧元缺⼝，其中
44 亿来⾃宏观环境恶化，仅利息⽀出就要多掏 36 亿。这还是基准情景。如果战事拖⻓，进⼊ " 严

### Page 6

重情景 " ，法国 2026 年增⻓为零， 2027 年萎缩 0.1% ，通胀在 2026 年冲到 4% 。
所以 " 中东通胀 " 同时打到法国的三个地⽅：家庭的账单、政府的⽀出、央⾏的利率。
⻢克⻰⻜往海湾的那天，预算案还在议会等他
10 ⽉ 18 ⽇晚，⻢克⻰发表电视讲话，谴责暴⼒，说 " 任何理由都不能为这些⾏为开脱 " 。他也承
认，抗议反映了⺠众对⽣活成本和油价的担忧，并要求警⽅对学⽣保持 " 相称 " 。
第⼆天，他⻜往科威特、阿联酋、巴林、卡塔尔，去谈⻓期的油⽓供应协议。
这个⾏程安排本⾝就是⼀幅画：他能管住外⾯的油轮，管不住⾥⾯的选票。
摆在议会桌上的是 540 亿欧元的财政整顿⽅案，其中 430 亿是 2027 年新增的。内容包括冻结公务
员⼯资的指数化（⼯会说这已是连续第四年）、冻结除最低档以外的养⽼⾦、压缩地⽅政府⽀出
和医保开⽀、下调企业社保缴费的税收优惠，还有把退休⼈员的税收减免上限从 4439 欧元降到
3000 欧元。国防是唯⼀的例外，明年还要再加 64 亿。

勒科尔尼的政府在国⺠议会没有多数。⽅案要在 12 ⽉前过，只有两条路：拿到⾜够多的妥协
票，或者动⽤宪法第 49.3 条强⾏通过，然后承受不信任投票。
荷兰的 ABN AMRO 给出的基准判断是： 540 亿⾥最后能活下来 350 到 400 亿，实际落地 250 到 300
亿， 2027 年的⾚字率还是 5.4% 左右。
过去两年，已经有两任总理因为预算被赶下台。勒科尔尼是第三个。
他在电视讲话⾥说了⼀句很实在的话： " 这是国家疲惫的时刻。我们正处在⼀场永久危机的氛围
⾥：战争、通胀、购买⼒。 "
明年 4 ⽉ 18 ⽇，才是这场危机的定价⽇
法国总统选举⾸轮定在 2027 年 4 ⽉ 18 ⽇，第⼆轮 5 ⽉ 2 ⽇。⻢克⻰不能再选。
9 ⽉的 Ipsos ⺠调显⽰，他的⽀持率是 16% ， 81% 的受访者对他的施政表现持负⾯评价。同⼀时
期的多份⺠调⾥，勒庞在⾸轮拿到 33% 到 36% ，梅朗雄和菲利普在争第⼆名。
债券市场现在要价的，是⼀个概率：明年之后，法国还有没有⼈敢削⾚字。⾄于候选⼈的政策

细节，反倒没多少⼈细看。
勒庞的⽅案是把公共债务上限写进法律，定在 GDP 的 60% ，同时降低部分⼯种的退休年龄、下
调能源税。梅朗雄的⽅案是让欧洲央⾏免掉⼀部分法国国债的利息。在多数市场⼈⼠看来，前
者会把法国直接推⼊衰退，后者在欧元区的法律框架⾥⾛不通。
市场其实不太在意谁当选。它在意的是，⽆论谁当选，紧缩都会被打折。凯投宏观的⾸席欧洲
经济学家肯宁厄姆在报告⾥写得很直⽩：投资者还担⼼选举后出现更强的财政⺠粹主义， " 利差
在选举前或选举后⼤幅⾛阔的⻛险都很⼤ " 。
这不是法国⼀家的事。 10 ⽉初，欧元跌到 1.12 美元， 2025 年 5 ⽉以来最弱。欧洲央⾏ 6 ⽉ 11 ⽇已
经加息 25 个基点，市场还在押年底前再加⼀到两次。意⼤利的利差反⽽⽐法国窄，罗⻢在这场
⻛暴⾥成了旁观者。

### Page 7

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

欧元区的可能性⾮常⼤，可能引发整个地区的严重危机 " 。
接下来盯四个⽇⼦

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건

목표주가(12M) 860,000 원(유지) 
종가(2026.09.10) 453,500 원 
상승여력 89.6 %

HD현대중공업, 중형엔진 및 SMR 시설투자 공시  
기다리던 엔진 증설의 세부 사항이 드디어 공개되었다. HD 현대중공업은 10일 공시를 통해 총 1조 722억원(중형엔진 
8,336억원/SMR 2,386억원) 규모의 시설투자 계획을 밝혔다. 먼저 중형엔진 관련, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 
확대에 대응 목적으로 생산능력 확충을 위한 토지 취득 및 공장 신설 및 생산설비 구축에 28년 5월까지 투자가 진행될 예정이다. 
중형엔진의 생산 능력은 현재 연간 3GW급에서 증설이 완료되면 7.2GW급으로 확장되며, 첫 엔진 출하는 28년 하반기로 예상된다. 
증설되는 신공장의 가동률은 28년 30%로 출발하여 30년 100%로 올라설 전망이다. 이에 따라 연간 1GW당 약 1조원의 매출과 
20%이상의 영업이익(현 엔진사업부 이상) 수준으로 추정되며, 계획대로 증설이 완료되고 일감을 확보한다면 총 4조원 이상의 매출 
증대 효과가 기대된다. SMR 투자 관련해서는 SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주물량 증가에 대응하기 위해 SMR 전용 공장, 설비 
구축 및 기존 공장 최적화에 29년 4월까지 2,386억원이 투입된다. 이는 연간으로 Terrapower의 Natrium SMR 주기기 2기(각 
345MW)를 제작할 수 있는 규모다. HD현대중공업은 이번 증설이 데이터센터용 엔진만을 위한 것이 아님을 강조했다. 데이터센터 뿐 
아니라 국가전력망, 원전용 비상발전기 등 전세계적으로 증가하는 육상용 발전엔진 수요에 대응하기 위함이며, 최근 미국의 육상 
데이터센터 사업이 님비현상 등으로 일부 좌초되고 있음에도 그보다 더 큰 그림에 베팅한 것이다.  
엔진도 끌어올리지 못하는 조선업 주가, 그래도 펀더멘털은 굳건  
분명한 기업가치 증분의 근거가 되는 증설공시였음에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업의 주가는 발표 이후 하락으로 마감하였다. 올해 
조선업 주가는 YTD 평균 10%이상 하락하며 약세를 보이고 있는 상태다. (HD현대중공업 -10.0%, 삼성중공업 -11.6%, 한화오션 -
25.5%)  
조선사 공히 2분기의 실적 서프라이즈와 함께 올해 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하는 등 우수한 펀더멘털을 유지하고 있으나 이는 
주가 상승재료로 반영되고 있지 않다. 7~8월 반도체발 주식시장 혼란에 따른 전체적 수급 약세도 무시할 수 없는 요인이다. 여기에 
지난해 주가 상승의 주요 동력으로 작용하였던 미국 함정 사업 관련 협력 진전이 느린 점 등이 주가 약세의 주요 원인으로 
분석된다. 백악관 및 美해군에서는 지속적으로 함정 해외 건조의 필요성을 역설하고 있으나 美입법부에서는 지역구 의원의 반대 
등으로 관련 법안의 입법 동력이 약해져 있는 상황이다. 美국회의 이번 회기(119대 회기, ~2027년 1월 3일까지)가 이미 막바지에 
다다르고 있으며 11월 중간선거 일정까지 감안하면 실질적으로 이번 회기중에 관련 법안이 통과 및 발효될 가능성은 미미하기 
때문에, 2026년 남은 하반기에 함정사업 관련 조선업 주가 동력을 기대하기는 힘들어졌다. 다가오고 있는 3분기 실적도 원화 강세 
및 여름휴가/사고 등에 따른 조업일수 하락, 후판 유통가 상승 등 감익 요인이 산적해 있어 2분기에 비해 쉽지 않은 상황이다.  
그러나 여전히 본업인 조선업의 체력은 훌륭하다. 전세계 선박 발주량은 8월 누적기준 5,972만 CGT로 전년동기 대비 +60.6% 많은 
발주가 나오고 있으며 이는 2021년 이후 가장 강한 발주세다. 한국 조선사의 주력 선종인 LNG, 탱커, 컨테이너선의 발주 비중이 
전체 발주량의 67%를 차지하고 있으며 이에 힘입어 조선사들은 수주목표를 사실상 초과 달성하였다. (상선기준, HD현대중공업 달성 
130.6억 달러/목표 114.7억, 삼성중공업 달성 61억/목표 57억, 한화오션 달성 60억/목표없음) 3분기 실적은 다소 쉬어갈 수 
있으나 여전히 수주잔고에 기반한 추정상 매출/영업이익은 2028년까지 꾸준한 상승이 예상된다. 작년보다 오히려 하락한 주가가 
현저한 저평가일 수밖에 없는 이유다. 조선업 비중확대 및 HD현대중공업 Top Pick 의견을 유지한다.

year's growth target on track. September data showed signs of
stabilisation, though domestic demand remains

up from 4.3% in the second quarter, a little stronger than expected

### Page 2

(market: 4.4%, ING: 4.4%). This brings the year-to-date GDP growth
down to 4.6% YoY.
By industry, China's tertiary sector continued to grow fastest for
the sixth consecutive quarter, up 5.2% YoY in 3Q, versus 3.6% and
3.4% for the primary and secondary industries, respectively. As
China's economy matures, the services sector will play an even
larger role.
A strong third-quarter trade surplus drove China's rebound. Final
consumption expenditure's contribution to GDP, at 2.148pp, was at

its lowest since 4Q22.
With a few months left in the year, we believe China's economy is
well positioned to achieve its 2026 growth target of 4.5-5.0%.
However, the economy remains quite unbalanced heading into
2027, leaving growth vulnerable to disruptions in external demand.
Fortunately, we also saw some favourable signs from the
September domestic activity data.
GDP growth should end the year on target
September's domestic data showed signs of

stabilisation
September's domestic activity data generally improved from
August, contributing to the 3Q26 GDP outperformance.
Below are our takeaways from the major data releases:

### Page 3

Retail sales growth was stronger than forecasts (market: 0.5%,
ING: 0.6%), rising to 1.1% YoY from 0.4%. Strong
communication appliance (31.1%) sales drove the data, likely
beneﬁting from a host of new phone model launches. Beyond
this category, sales of consumer staples such as tobacco and
alcohol (13.8%), grains & oils (6.7%), and beverages (5.0%) all
outperformed. However, many discretionary consumption
categories, such as gold and jewellery (-9.6%) and autos

(-21.2%), remained weak.
Fixed asset investment growth stabilised at -7.2% YoY after
decelerating every month since March, coming in stronger than
expectations (market: -7.3%, ING: -7.5%). This stabilisation
reﬂects public-led (-3.2%) and manufacturing (-2.1%) FAI
picking up on the month, oﬀsetting slowdowns in private-led
(-10.4%), real estate (-20.6%), and infrastructure (-4.2%) FAI.
Investment has clearly been a major drag this year, so
stabilisation is encouraging, possibly as eﬀorts to accelerate

ﬁscal spending outlined at July's Politburo meeting begin to
take eﬀect.
Industrial production continues to be the main outperformer,
up 5.5% YoY from 5.2% in August, in line with market
expectations (market: 5.5%, ING: 5.5%). Hi-tech manufacturing
(15.3%) remains a bright spot, and export-oriented industries
such as computers and communications (16.4%), rail, ships,
and aerospace (11.7%), and autos (6.7%) continue to
outperform.
70-city property prices continued to drop, but by less than

what we saw in August's data. New home prices (-0.14% MoM)
fell the least in 17 months. Prices stabilised or rose in 21 cities.
Existing home prices (-0.26% MoM) saw the smallest drop in 4
months, with prices stabilising or rising in 11 cities. Tier 1 cities
continued to perform well on the month, with a few tier 2 cities
joining the price recovery.
Compared with rapid export growth, domestic activity continues to
lag. But September's data at least shows modest positive

momentum heading into 4Q26.

Document 294: Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds

Source PDF: all report pdf/Compelling risk-reward in US 10-year bonds.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

Compelling risk-reward in 
US 10-year bonds  
Summary 
Event: The US 10 -year government bond yield has surged 
past 5.0% to a 19-year high and has now entered our three -
month forecast range of 5.0-5.25%. At the time of writing, the 
10-year yield is trading at 5.2%, its highest since June 2007’s 
high of 5.32%. The next resistance levels are the 2002 high 
of 5.47% and the 2001 high of 5.53%.  
We view the risk/reward for the US 10-year government 
bond after the recent yield spike as  compelling and we 
are initiating a long opportunistic idea at the current 
yield. As the table on the right shows, if the 10 -year yield 
rises to 6%, investors would face less than 1% in negative 
total returns over a one-year period, whereas if the yield falls 
below 5.0%, they would gain at least 6% in total returns.  
While we continue to prefer the 3-7-year bond maturity range 
for income generation (the US 5-year bond yield is also 
above 5%), and broadly prefer corporate over government 
bonds amid solid corporate fundamentals, the US 10 -year 
government bond yield has finally reached reasonable levels 
for investors looking for slightly longer maturities. 
Rationale: We expect the US 10-year government bond yield 
to ease to 4.75-5.0% over a 12-month period due to: 
Easing oil prices and inflation over next 12 months: While 
the yield is likely to remain elevated in the next three months 
due to elevated oil prices caused by geopolitical uncertainty, 
heavy AI investment-driven bond issuance and a resilient US 
economy, it is likely to ease towards the 4.75 -5.0% range 
over 12  months as oil prices moderate either due to a 
resolution in the Middle East or a rerouting of Middle East 
energy supplies and as the impact of tariffs fade.  
We expect s hallower Fed rate hikes than market s: We 
expect a total of 50bps of Fed rate hikes by June 2027, before 
a cut in H2 as US inflation eventually eases towards the Fed’s 
2% target. This  rate trajectory is shallower than c. 95bps of 
further hikes priced by markets over the next 12 months. 
 The benchmark US 10-year government bond yield has 
risen to its highest since 2007, offering medium-term 
investors a compelling risk-reward trade-off 
US 10-year government bond yield and previous highs

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Investor positioning adjustment: In the very near-term, the 
US 10 -year bond yield has room to move lower as hedge 
funds using systematic strategies (CTAs) are extremely short 
US government bonds, raising the chance of short -covering 
if upcoming jobs and inflation data miss estimates. 
A shallow Fed hiking cycle  
We expect the shallowest Fed hiking cycle in almost 
three decades. This should enable the US 10-year yield to 
ease towards 4.75 -5.0% over a 12 -month period. Following 
this month’s increase, we forecast two further 25bp hikes by 
June 2027, followed by a cut in H2 2027. Markets price at 
least three more hikes by June and a strong probability of a 
fourth by September. Our projected 75bp s of total hikes this 
cycle compares with 525bps of hikes in 2022–23, 225bps in 
2015–18, 425bps in 2004–06 and 175bp in 1999–2000. The 
last smaller mid-cycle move was a single 25bps hike in 1997. 
Why we expect a more cautious Fed 
The latest hike restored the Fed’s credibility after renewed 
oil-price pressures, elevated inflation and a balanced labour 
market prompted a unanimous policy vote. However, the 
following factors argue against aggressive tightening: 
Supply-driven inflation: Energy costs and AI-infrastructure 
constraints, rather than excess demand, are driving prices. 
Higher rates cannot repair supply and may weaken growth. 
Labour is no longer the main inflation source:  Labour 
supply and demand are better balanced, wage growth has 
moderated and unemployment is near full employment. AI 
further weakens the traditional Phillips Curve relationship 
between unemployment and inflation. 
Disinflation should resume:  Energy pressures may fade 
after the US mid-term elections, while the one-off inflationary 
effects of tariffs should diminish after 2025–26. 
AI raises the neutral rate , but dampens inflation:  
Investment and productivity may lift the neutral rate ( r*), the 
rate consistent with stable inflation and full employment, 
while efficiency gains limit wage-price spirals. 
Markets are already tightening  financial conditions : 
Higher bond yields have raised borrowing costs and 
restrained activity, doing part of the Fed’s work. 
The cycle began from a higher base:  The Fed funds rate 
started at 3.75%, versus 0.25% in the previous two cycles 
and 1% before that, reducing additional tightening required. 
Monetary transmission has weakened:  Wealthier 
households drive more spending but are less rate -sensitive, 
while lower -income consumers are already constrained. 
Further hikes may , therefore, curb demand less effectively 
while causing disproportionate harm to low-income families. 
 We expect a shallower Fed rate hiking cycle compared 
with market expectations

### Page 3

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 28 September 2026

Small business conditions are deteriorating:  Higher 
financing and energy costs are weakening sentiment, as 
reflected in the latest Beige Book . The renewed 
underperformance of US small capitalisation (S&P 600) and 
mid capitalisation (S&P 400) equity indices  versus the large 
cap S&P 500 index over the past couple of months also show 
rising pressure on small businesses. 
Fiscal and financial -stability risks are rising:  Aggressive 
Fed tightening would increase US debt-servicing costs as the 
US Treasury seeks cheaper funding through shorter-maturity 
issuance, while potentially amplifying financial-system stress. 
Upside risk to bond yields 
Our view of lower bond yields carries the following risks: 
Persistent inflation:  Repeated supply shocks, strong AI -
related investment demand and tighter immigration controls 
could keep inflation above target and force steeper Fed rate 
hikes. With unemployment near the Fed’s estimate of full 
employment, a sharp drop in labour supply could intensify 
wage pressures and destabilise inflation expectations . We 
note that long-term expectations remain contained for now. 
Geopolitical escalation:  An intensification of geopolitical 
conflicts after the mid -term elections could lift energy prices 
and inflation. However, a Democrat -controlled or divided 
Congress may constrain US foreign-policy initiatives through 
tighter budgets, while Republicans could turn less supportive 
as attention shifts to the 2028 presidential race. 
Higher neutral rate: AI investments could raise the neutral 
rate (r*), turning current monetary policy settings 
accommodative. This could necessitate further rate hikes. 
However, the Fed has barely raised its nominal neutral rate 
estimate, lifting it to 3.2% in September from 3.0% a year ago. 
Bottomline: The outlook for rates remains geopolitics 
dependent, with all Fed meetings live . However, based on 
our core scenario, we see the US 10-year yield settling lower 
in the 4.75-5.0% in 6-12 months. A shallow Fed hiking cycle 
due to expected disinflation offers investors a reasonably 
attractive risk-reward in US 10-year government bonds. 
US 30-year government bonds , on the other hand, may 
continue to see elevated yields as the US fiscal deficit is 
unlikely to have improved next year, and may look much 
worse. As a result, “term premium” may keep the ultra -long 
end elevated. Short of some unorthodox policy to artifici ally 
cap them lower, the 30-year yield is likely to continue to drift 
higher towards the 5.5-5.75% range into H2 2027. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist 
Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

Document 313: Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Energy shock_ Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022 - October 2026 (2026-10-02).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Energy shock: Dashboard 2026 vs. 2022
10/2/2026 2
Will the same causes produce the same effects? In other words, will the war in Iran and the resulting
surge in oil and gas prices lead to an inflationary shock comparable to that seen in 2022? Will their
negative effects on growth be the same as those of the war in Ukraine and the subsequent energy
shock?
We have selected a set of indicators to track the impact of this new energy shock — caused by the war

in the Middle East — on activity and prices in the Eurozone, the United States, oil and gas markets and
emerging countries, and to see how much the current situation resembles that of 2022 at the outbreak
of the conflict in Ukraine.
This dashboard features charts and comments that will be updated on a monthly basis for as long as
necessary.
Overall, based on data available through September 2026, economic activity continues to show
resilience. The high levels of business climate surveys illustrate this well. Household confidence,

however, is more vulnerable and sensitive to energy price pressures. Financing conditions in emerging
economies also continue to hold up well against the shock (much better than in 2022) as well as
against the tensions in DM bond markets, which intensified this summer, partly in the wake of the
reinforcing upward pressure on energy prices. Higher energy prices are also fueling inflationary
pressures and pushing inflation even higher. In particular, Eurozone inflation rose by 0.6 percentage

points to 3.8% year-on-year (September flash estimate), reaching its highest level in three years.

to a 3-year high

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKET CALL: Downgrading 'Go Global' To Market Weight

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Downgrading 'Go Global' To
Market Weight
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 18 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Robert Nyman / Unsplash
We are downgrading the “Go Global” portfolio strategy from overweight to
market weight and raising “Stay Home” (in the US) from underweight to
market weight.
Overseas earnings remain impressive, but the rallies in emerging market
economies’ stock markets have been led by just a handful of AI-related
stocks. Last week's rout in South Korea showed how vulnerable such
concentration leaves these stock markets. Additionally, European

economies look set to struggle as France's fiscal problems loom, and
China's economy depends almost entirely on exports. Plus, the Middle
East war could flare up again before or after the US midterm elections on
November 3, keeping oil prices, inflation, and interest rates elevated.
Here's more:
I. Global Bonds
The global bond rout took a breather last week. Yields fell in all eight
major bond markets we track, led by the US (chart). The US 10-year

Treasury yield fell 16bps to 5.08%, even as the CPI rose 3.7% y/y and the
PPI jumped 6.3% in September. That suggests bond investors see these
rates as attractive relative to their long-term inflation expectations.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields has stabilized at
around 137bps, near its recent high (chart). Spreads for Italy, Greece,

### Page 3

Spain, and Portugal have eased slightly from their late-September peaks.
Contagion from France has eased for now. But France's own fiscal risk
premium hasn't. The National Assembly began debating France's 2027
budget on October 13. Meanwhile, Paris is burning!
II. Global Stocks
Brazil leads the country ETF rankings mtd, up 12.3% in dollars, ahead of
next Sunday's presidential runoff (chart). South Korea is at the bottom,
down 17.7%. The US is up 0.1%, ahead of the All Country World ex-US

ETF, down 1.9%.

### Page 4

Europe's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has fallen to
13.0%, from as high as 30.8% in 2007 (chart). It looks set to keep losing
ground for a while. The US MSCI’s share has firmed to 64.8%, up from
62.4% in March. The Emerging Markets MSCI’s share has held at 12.1%
despite the Korean selloff.
The ratio of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI has turned
up in recent weeks (chart). It remains below its early-2025 high.

### Page 5

III. Earnings & Valuation
The All Country World ex US MSCI's forward earnings is up 41.4% y/y,
while forward revenues is up 11.0% (chart). The global economy is doing
well.
Overseas, earnings have far outpaced stock prices this year. The All
Country World ex-US MSCI is up 10.5% ytd, while its forward earnings is
up 35.9% (chart). Its forward P/E has dropped 18.9% ytd.

### Page 6

The US MSCI's forward P/E is 19.1 versus 12.2 for the All Country World
ex-US, a 57% premium (chart). US profit margins justify about half of it.
The US forward profit margin is 16.8%, about a third higher than the All
Country World ex-US's 12.7%.
IV. China Focus
China remains a source of deflation for the global economy through its
massive trade surplus. Exports jumped 36.3% y/y in September to a
record high (chart). The trade surplus was $133.5 billion. Semiconductor

exports more than doubled during the first nine months of the year.
Booming exports are the only reason China's economy isn't imploding.

### Page 7

The domestic economy remains weak, weighed down by a rapidly aging
population and a property debacle. The Shenzhen Real Estate Stock
Price Index is testing a floor that has held since 2012 (chart). We would
worry if it broke below that level.
The stock market hasn't benefited from the export boom either, so Beijing
appears to be propping it up again. Regulators met on Thursday to
discuss stabilization measures, according to Bloomberg. They reportedly

urged companies and state funds to buy shares and asked some
investors to curb selling. Mainland A-shares, where Beijing's state-backed
funds do most of their buying, held up into mid-2026 before turning down
(chart). Offshore Chinese stocks have been falling since late 2025.

clause.

Document 398: The Currency Book (2026-09-29)

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

The Currency Book

1 
Authors 
George Saravelos

September 29, 2026 
Introduction 
With trillions of dollars, euros and yen changing hands every day, foreign 
exchange is indisputably the largest and most liquid financial market in the 
world. Yet despite its size, the currency market is also one of the least 
understood. Unlike bonds or equities, there is no widely accepted framework for 
explaining currency moves, which reflect a complex interplay of 
macroeconomics, capital flows, market structure, investor behaviour and 
(geo)politics. 
Over the past two decades, advances in technology and market infrastructure 
have transformed how currencies are traded, placing FX at the forefront of 
broader changes in global finance. Yet because currencies sit at the intersection 
of national economies, the FX market is also fundamentally connected to 
international trade and cross-border capital flows. It is therefore increasingly 
exposed to the forces reshaping the global economic landscape—from 
geopolitical fragmentation and changing trade relationships to shifts in 
regulation, payments infrastructure and the architecture of the international 
monetary system. 
This Deutsche Bank Currency Book brings together academic, industry and 
Deutsche Bank expertise to explain how FX markets function, how exchange 
rates are determined, how investors seek to generate returns from currencies, 
and how FX risk can be managed by investors and corporates alike. Our aim 
throughout the Book is to set out the enduring principles driving markets 
alongside the developments defining FX today. Ultimately, the structure of the FX 
market is not simply a question of how currencies trade, but of who controls the 
financial infrastructure through which money moves across borders. The 
evolution of FX is increasingly intertwined with questions of monetary 
sovereignty, financial resilience and geopolitical power. 
Chapter 1 reviews the structure of the FX market, examining its participants, 
ecosystem and evolution over time. Chapter 2 discusses exchange rate 
determination, moving from traditional academic theories to modern macro-
financial approaches and practical decision frameworks. Chapter 3 reviews FX 
as an asset class and the evidence on currency investing, while Chapter 4 
considers currency hedging for both investors and corporates. Finally, Chapter 5 
discusses the future of FX, including the impact of technology, payment systems 
and digital assets.

Introduction
With trillions of dollars, euros and yen changing hands every day, foreign exchange
is indisputably the largest and most liquid financial market in the world. Yet despite
its size, the currency market is also one of the least understood. Unlike bonds or
equities, there is no widely accepted framework for explaining currency moves,
which reflect a complex  interplay of macroeconomics,  capital flows,  market
structure, investor behaviour and (geo)politics.

Over the past two decades, advances in technology and market infrastructure have
transformed how currencies are traded, placing FX at the  forefront of broader
changes in global finance. Yet because currencies sit at the intersection of national
economies, the FX market is also fundamentally connected to international trade
and cross-border capital flows. It is therefore increasingly exposed to the forces
reshaping the global economic landscape—from geopolitical fragmentation and

changing trade relationships to shifts in regulation, payments infrastructure and
the architecture of the international monetary system.
This Deutsche Bank Currency Book brings together  academic, industry and
Deutsche Bank expertise to explain how FX markets function, how exchange rates
are determined, how investors seek to generate returns from currencies, and how
FX risk can be managed by investors and corporates alike. Our aim throughout
the Book is to set out the enduring principles driving markets alongside

the developments defining FX today. Ultimately, the structure of the FX
market is not simply a question of how currencies trade, but of who
controls the financial infrastructure through which money moves across
borders. The evolution of FX is increasingly intertwined with questions
of monetary sovereignty, financial resilience and geopolitical power.

### Page 7

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 7
Introduction
The foreign exchange market is the world’s largest and most liquid financial market,
but it differs fundamentally from a centralised exchange. Trading takes place across
a fragmented, largely over-the-counter network of banks, non-bank liquidity
providers, electronic venues and end-users, using instruments ranging from spot
and forwards to swaps, futures and options. How do transactions move through

this network? Who provides liquidity, who consumes it and what risks arise
between execution and settlement? This chapter maps the FX ecosystem from
instruments and trading infrastructure to the participants ultimately responsible for
currency demand.
1.1 Instruments
The FX market is not one single product. Instead, it is made up of a group of
instruments that allow market participants to exchange currencies today, lock in an
exchange rate for the future and/or hedge against adverse exchange-rate moves.

Below, we outline the main instruments in FX markets and their users.
Spot: This is the simplest and most widely known FX instrument. When market
participants refer to “the exchange-rate” or the “spot exchange-rate,” they
generally mean the price agreed today for exchanging one currency for another on
the standard spot settlement date. For most major currency pairs spot settlement
is two business days later (T+2).1  For example, a spot purchase of EUR 1 million

against the USD at a EUR/USD exchange-rate of 1.20 means that the buyer receives
EUR 1 million in his or her bank account on the settlement date and the seller
receives USD 1.2 million from the buyer on the same date.
1 There are exceptions, for example USD/CAD settles T+1.

### Page 8

The Currency Book
Page 8 Deutsche Bank AG
By convention, the first currency in an exchange-rate quotation is known as the
base currency, while the second is the quote currency. The exchange-rate therefore
represents the number of units of the quote currency required to purchase one unit
of the base currency. In USD/JPY, for example, the USD is the base currency and the
exchange-rate indicates the number of Japanese yen required to purchase one US

dollar. A USD/JPY exchange-rate of 160 means 1 USD can be exchanged for 160
JPY.
Spot transactions are the most commonly used foreign exchange payments for
cross-border payments for goods and services, the purchase of foreign financial
assets, as well as portfolio rebalancing and proprietary trading. In 2025, the Bank
for International Settlements (BIS) estimates spot turnover at $3trn per day,
accounting for 31% of global over-the-counter (OTC) FX turnover.

Forwards and futures: An (outright) forward is similar to a spot transaction, but is
instead an agreement to exchange two currencies at a rate (price) agreed today, but
with settlement taking place at a date more than T+2 days in the future. They are
also traded OTC. Forwards are most commonly used by those who have a known
future cash flow and want to remove uncertainty around the future exchange rate.
In 2025, the BIS estimates that they account for 19% of global turnover, or $1.8trn

per day.
Futures are economically similar to forwards but are standardised contracts traded
on exchanges and centrally cleared. This provides transparent pricing and reduces
bilateral counterparty risk, but offers less flexibility over contract size, maturity and
settlement terms than an OTC forward. Exchange-traded FX futures remain
considerably smaller than the OTC FX market.
Non-deliverable forwards (NDFs): An NDF is a cash-settled forward commonly

used for currencies where convertibility or offshore deliverability is restricted. Two
parties agree today on an exchange rate for a future date, but unlike a standard
forward, the underlying currencies are not exchanged. At maturity, the agreed NDF
rate is compared with a pre-specified fixing or reference rate. The difference is
usually settled in USD. NDFs are commonly used by corporates, banks and
investors to hedge or take exposure to currencies subject to capital controls.

Swaps: An FX swap combines two transactions: an exchange of currencies on an
initial date and a reverse exchange at a later date, with both exchange rates agreed
when the swap is initiated. They are used by institutions that hold cash in one
currency (e.g. EUR) but need funding in another (e.g. USD) for investment, hedging
or other needs. They are by far the largest FX instrument by turnover, with the BIS
estimating turnover of $4trn per day in April 2025, 42% of global FX turnover. A

swap is, in effect, a form of collateralised borrowing, with the collateral being cash
in a different currency to that of the currency borrowed.
Cross-currency (XCCY) swaps: A cross-currency swap is similar to an FX swap in
that principal amounts denominated in two different currencies are typically
exchanged at inception and reversed at maturity. The key difference is that a cross-
currency swap also involves periodic exchanges of interest payments denominated

in the two currencies over the life of the contract. These payments may be fixed or
floating. Cross-currency swaps therefore allow market participants to transform
both the currency denomination and, where relevant, the interest-rate
characteristics of an asset or liability.

### Page 9

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 9
For example:
n Suppose a European corporate issues a USD-denominated bond to US-
based investors. It receives the bond proceeds in USD, but must
periodically pay interest in USD and repay the USD principal when the bond
matures. However, because the corporate primarily earns revenue in EUR,
it needs to convert these USD liabilities into EUR liabilities to avoid currency
and potentially interest-rate risk.

n The corporate removes FX and interest-rate risk by entering into a xccy
swap, where, in essence, it converts this USD-liability into a EUR-liability.2
n They are commonly used by banks, corporates and investors that need to
raise money in one currency but need funds in another. The basis is the extra
spread added to one side of the swap to balance unequal demand for
funding in the two currencies. See Section 2.11 for more on cross-currency
basis and here.

Options: An option gives the holder the right, but not the obligation, to buy or sell
one currency against another at a specified exchange rate during a specified period.
Options are used to hedge currency risk or speculate on currency movements and
are particularly useful when the end-user wants an asymmetric payoff profile.
Unlike a forward, an option can limit losses from an adverse currency movement
while allowing the holder to retain some or all of the benefit from a favourable

movement. In 2025, they represented around 7% of global FX turnover. Options are
widely used by corporates and investors for hedging, and by investors and dealers
to express directional or volatility views.
The simplest and most widely used instruments are vanilla options, which give the
holder the right to buy (a call) or sell (a put) a currency at a predetermined strike
price. A European-style option can be exercised only at expiry, while an American-

style option can be exercised at any point up to expiry. More structured products
include barrier options, whose payoff is activated or extinguished if the exchange
rate reaches a specified level—for example, knock-in and knock-out options. One-
touch and no-touch options pay a fixed amount depending on whether the
exchange-rate does, or does not, reach a predetermined level during the life of the
option. These instruments are commonly used to express views not just on the

direction of a currency pair, but also on the probability, timing and magnitude of
exchange-rate moves.
1.2 FX spot trading life cycle and risks
Having discussed the main FX instruments, the next step is to examine how these
transactions are executed and settled in practice. The complete trade life cycle,
meaning the sequence of events and processes involved in executing a trade, is
crucial to understanding how FX transactions work. We therefore describe below

the broad steps involved in trading FX, focusing on spot transactions. They are:
1.  Pre-trade: This stage encompasses all the necessary preparations before
a trade is executed, including client onboarding, platform selection, credit
checks, and regulatory compliance.
2 Specifically, it exchanges the USD bond proceeds for EUR at inception, receives USD interest payments
(from the swap counterparty to pay U.S. bond investors) and pays EUR interest (to the swap

counterparty) over the life of the swap, and receives USD principal (from the swap counterparty to pay
U.S. investors) in exchange for EUR principal at maturity. The USD receipts from the swap are used to
meet the bond’s USD interest and principal payments, effectively converting the USD-denominated
bond into a EUR-denominated liability.

### Page 10

The Currency Book
Page 10 Deutsche Bank AG
2.  Trade execution: This is the point where the trade is agreed upon and
executed at a specific exchange rate, creating a legally binding contract.
3.  Trade clearing: This stage involves validating trade details and managing
counterparty credit risk before settlement, either bilaterally or through a
central clearing counterparty (CCP). In centrally cleared FX markets, the
CCP becomes the buyer to every seller and the seller to every buyer,

reducing direct counterparty exposures between market participants.
Specialist FX CCPs, such as LCH's ForexClear, clear both non-deliverable
and deliverable FX products. While FX spot transactions can be cleared,
they are not subject to mandatory clearing requirements. ForexClear
supports 25 non-deliverable forward (NDF) currency pairs, 9 non-
deliverable option (NDO) currency pairs, and 8 deliverable (Spot, Forwards
and Options) currency pairs, at the time of writing.

4.  Trade settlement: This final stage represents the completion of the
transaction, with the actual exchange of currencies taking place.
Settlement for spot trades typically occurs two business days (T+2) after
the trade date, although exceptions exist. For example, USD/CAD settles
on a T+1 basis.
Figure 2: FX Spot Trade Life Cycle
Source : Deutsche Bank
Risks surrounding FX spot trading
The over-the-counter (OTC) nature of the FX market, while offering flexibility and

adaptability to participant needs, does present certain risks that are often more
pronounced compared to exchange-traded markets. Below we discuss some of the
key risks in FX.
Credit risk: Credit risk in FX trading, stemming from a counterparty's potential
inability to fulfill its obligations, is present throughout the trade lifecycle:
n Pre-trade: Counterparty creditworthiness is a critical consideration in the
A Central Clearing Counterparty (CCP), also

known as a Central Counterparty, stands
between buyers and sellers in financial
markets, becoming the buyer to every
seller and the seller to every buyer. This
mitigates counterparty risk.

### Page 11

The Currency Book
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
pre-trade stage of FX transactions. A thorough assessment should involve
evaluating the potential counterparty's financial strength, credit rating, and
reputation. Choosing a creditworthy counterparty significantly reduces the
risk of default throughout the trade lifecycle.
n Post-trade (Clearing and Settlement): While credit risk exists in both the
clearing (post-execution/pre-settlement) and settlement stages of a spot

FX trade, it primarily materializes during settlement. This stage, involving
the actual exchange of currencies, carries the highest risk due to the
potential for settlement failure. This risk, often referred to as settlement risk,
arises when one party delivers their currency while the counterparty
defaults. Cross-border trades are particularly vulnerable due to time zone
differences, which can create a window where one party has performed but
the other has not. Additionally, mark-to-market risk from FX rate

fluctuations between trade and settlement dates can exacerbate
settlement risk by increasing potential losses from a default. This is
particularly pronounced with forward contracts, where the longer pre-
settlement period leads to greater mark-to-market risk and, consequently,
higher settlement risk.
Market Risk: This risk in FX trading arises from potential losses due to adverse
exchange-rate movements. This is influenced by factors like economic data,

geopolitical events, and interest rates. For customers, asymmetric information,
where one party has more or better information than another, can lead to inefficient
pricing and increased volatility, thereby exacerbating market risk.
n For dealers, a key component of market risk is inventory management risk
– the potential for losses from unfavourable price movements in the
currencies they hold to facilitate client trading. Effective inventory
management, including position limits and hedging, mitigates this risk.

Liquidity Risk: This refers to the overall market's ability to facilitate the buying and
selling of a currency pair quickly and efficiently at a desired price. It's influenced by
factors such as market depth, trading volume, volatility, and fragmentation.
Operational Risk: This encompasses risks associated with trade processing,
settlement, and technology. Errors in trade execution, system failures, and fraud
can all lead to significant losses.
Regulatory Risk: This refers to the risk arising from changes in regulations or their

interpretation. The evolving regulatory landscape in FX, particularly concerning
non-bank dealers and systemic risk, can impact market participants and create
uncertainty.
1.3 Market participants
The FX market comprises a variety of participants with different objectives. FX
market participants fall into two broad categories: financial and non-financial.
Financial participants can act as both liquidity providers and consumers, while non-
financial participants act largely as liquidity consumers.

Financial participants: In the FX market, financial participants are primarily banks
acting as dealers.
n Dealers: These are mainly large commercial banks, investment banks and
securities houses that participate in the inter-dealer market for proprietary

### Page 12

The Currency Book
Page 12 Deutsche Bank AG
trading. 3 and/or for meeting customer demand through market-making.4
n Institutional investors: These are mutual funds, pension funds, insurance
companies and endowment funds, collectively referred to as “real-money
investors”, which need to buy currencies to satisfy end-user demand for an
underlying asset. For instance, a UK pension fund needs to buy USD to
purchase US equities. These investors also engage in hedging, proprietary

trading and risk management activities.
n Hedge Funds & Principal Trading Firms (PTFs): Hedge funds are
investment funds that follow a broad range of investment strategies and are
generally not subject to the same borrowing and leverage restrictions as
traditional investment funds. They are users of FX and derivatives for
speculative purposes. PTFs are principal trading firms, such as XTX
Markets and Citadel Securities, that hedge or speculate for their own

account and also participate in market-making. This category may include
high-frequency trading firms that employ high-speed algorithmic trading
strategies.
Non financial institutions: Non-financial participants, such as corporations
involved in international trade, retail and the official sector, also primarily consume
liquidity.
n Corporates are active in currency markets primarily to manage FX
exposures 5  arising from international trade, foreign-currency financing,

overseas operations and cross-border mergers and acquisitions (M&A).6
n Official sector: This category includes central banks, sovereign wealth
funds, international financial institutions, such as the IMF and BIS,
development banks and government agencies. Monetary-policy, financial-
stability and mercantilist considerations have all, at various times,
motivated central-bank intervention in FX.7
n Other: This category includes all other market participants, including retail

participants. Retail participants engage in FX both to speculate and to meet
transactional demand arising from travel, trade or investment.
1.4 Profit versus non-profit seekers
In 2025, daily FX turnover reached $9.5 trillion according to the BIS triennial survey
(BIS, 2025) making currency trading the world's largest financial market. Yet,
size and liquidity do not necessarily imply efficiency. Unlike most equity or
bond transactions, a large share of FX activity serves an underlying

transactional purpose. For instance, corporates must convert revenues and
meet foreign- currency invoices; institutional investors hedge overseas
assets; and dealers intermediate these flows. For these participants,
expected FX returns may be secondary, or even irrelevant, to the decision to
trade. In this section, we investigate
3 Proprietary trading is a profit-seeking activity that dealers once engaged in more extensively, but which
has been severely constrained by regulatory change. Most notably, the Volcker Rule, implemented in

2014-15 under the Dodd-Frank regulations, bans banks from engaging in proprietary trading activities,
including many foreign exchange instruments.
4 Market making: This is an activity that seeks to capture returns by matching buyers and sellers and
charging a price for the service through the bid-offer spread. This spread represents the price that
liquidity seekers must pay to access the market and is a “frictional” cost of executing transactions. As

such, market-making is a liquidity-provision service rather than a source of the ‘excess returns'
associated with activities such as proprietary trading

(2) Mandate constraints relate to limits

constraints on investment allocations and
government policy (i.e. Norway's pension

government). These restrictions can
materially limit a fund’s ability to take
currency risk. The simplest mandate

funds, with the former following an index
and therefore being explicitly required to
track its returns.
(3) Process constraints relate to limits
placed on the decision-making process.
Most investment funds abide by internal

shocks.

Approach 1: The Triennial BIS Survey of FX market volumes
We use the 2025 BIS Triennial Survey to update Deutsche Bank's previous work

(Saravelos et al, 2018) and estimate the share of FX turnover initiated with a profit-
seeking currency objective. We construct a "realistic scenario", which applies what
we deem to be "realistic" assumptions about participants’ ability and mandate to
take FX risk.8 We find that profit-seeking activity accounts for approximately 40%
of turnover under the realistic scenario.9
Financial participants
n Dealers: Dealer-to-dealer activity accounts for approximately 47% of total

daily FX turnover ($4.44trn) and we classify 10% of dealer flow as profit-
seeking ($444bn/day). Given that much of the daily FX turnover reflects
market-making, we assume that inter-dealer transactions conducted by
voice are not profit-seeking and that only electronic transactions
conducted through order-matching systems such as EBS are profit-
seeking.
n Hedge Funds and PTFs: Hedge Funds and PTFs account for approximately
$760bn (8%) of headline daily FX turnover and we classify 90% of this as

profit-seeking ($684bn/day). The $684bn/day figure represents the profit-
seeking nature of hedge-funds where the remaining 10% reflects the
ensure that the mark-to-market value of their hedge does not introduce unwanted volatility in the
income statement. IFRS 9 provided an updated set of standards for hedge accounting, designed to
increase flexibility and better link financial reporting to firms’ risk management practices. However,
hedge accounting still places limitations on the types of instruments and kinds of hedges that can be

implemented. For example, to qualify for hedge accounting, corporates must adhere to hedge
effectiveness and demonstrate that an economic relationship between the underlying cash flow or
asset and the hedge exists, as well as quantitative limits to hedge ratios.
7 Exchange rates are explicitly targeted by some central banks as part of their overall monetary policy
framework (for instance the Monetary Authority of Singapore or the Hong Kong Monetary Authority);

other central banks have intervened to defend their inflation target or financial stability reasons (most
recently, the Swiss National Bank); and others to prevent competitiveness losses in global trade.

expansion of PTFs to act not only as profit-seekers but also as liquidity
providers.

n Non-Reporting Banks: Non-reporting banks account for approximately
$2.19trn, or 23% of headline daily turnover and we classify 10% of this as
profit-seeking ($220bn/day). These largely intermediate for institutional

and non-financial customers and apply the same profit-seeking

n Institutional investors:Institutional investors account for $2.56tn of daily

profit-seeking share of approximately 30%.
n Official Sector: The official sector accounts for approximately 1-2% of

Approach 2: Commitment of Traders CFTC data
While the BIS data provide the most comprehensive view of the global FX market,
the survey is only updated every three years. For a higher-frequency perspective, we
therefore complement it with the US Commodity Futures Trading Commission’s
Commitments of Traders (CoT) report, which is published weekly and provides a

breakdown of positions in exchange-traded currency futures and options,
principally those traded on the Chicago Mercantile Exchange (CME). The report
sho
ws the number of outstanding long and short contracts held by different
categories of market participant, based on positions as of each Tuesday. Although
the futures market represents only a small subset of total global FX activity—most
of which takes place over the counter—the CoT data provide a timely and consistent

measure of how speculative and hedging positions are evolving.
11 Corporates use FX to settle foreign-currency revenues and expenses, finance cross-border activity,
hedge forecast cash flows and manage earnings volatility. Governments similarly transact to meet an
underlying funding or expenditure requirement.
12 Some investors trade currencies to earn returns, while other transactions relate to travel, remittances
or purchases of foreign assets

### Page 16

The Currency Book
Page 16 Deutsche Bank AG
In the traditional or “legacy” CoT report, reportable positions  are divided into
commercial and non-commercial traders, alongside a residual category of smaller,
non-reportable positions. Commercial traders are generally entities that use futures
primarily in connection  with an underlying business activity  and are therefore
commonly interpreted as hedgers—for  example, corporates or financial
institutions managing currency exposures. We classify these positions as liquidity

seeking. Non-commercial traders,  by contrast, are participants  whose futures
activity is not classified as commercial hedging and typically includes hedge funds,
commodity trading advisers and other leveraged investors. We therefore treat
non-commercial positions as profit-seeking. Figure 7 and Figure 8 show how the
relative size of the profit-seeking FX market has developed over time. Using one-
year rolling averages to reduce the noise of the data, we see that roughly 30-40%

of the futures market (EUR/USD, GBP/USD, USD/JPY) is profit-seeking.

Approach 3: Options market DTCC data
Following the implementation of the Dodd-Frank Act, OTC FX options falling within
the US regulatory perimeter are required to be reported to a CFTC-registered swap
data repository (SDR). This includes transactions involving US market participants,
among others. DTCC operates one of the largest data repositories and therefore

captures a substantial share of global institutional FX option activity. We estimate
that the DTCC data capture more than  20% of global FX derivative  volumes,
providing another way of estimating the relative importance of profit- and non-
profit-seeking activity in the FX market.
While the data does not provide  client granularity, we can back out the  likely
motivation of the transaction by distinguishing between out-of-the-money and in-
the-money option trades. We argue that strikes preferred by the different client

groups will likely differ, with hedgers favouring out of the money options (0-25
delta). While speculators focus on directional leverage, the latter often translates
to options with strikes nearer to the money (25-75 delta).
Figure 9 shows the split in options volumes for 2025/26 across the two investor
categories. We find that option volumes are roughly split equally between investors
and hedgers. FX options are instruments that are favoured by hedge funds over

corporates and institutional investors, so the results here likely materially overstate
the relative importance of profit-maximizing participants in other markets. Again,
this shows the FX market is split roughly 50/50 by those who are profit-seeking and
those who are not.
